{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_11/projects/phase-gated-compound-sparse/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — Phase-Gated Compound Sparsity\\n\\n> 最后更新: 2026-05-14 (D052 审稿应对调整) | 维护者: Director\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nCompound sparsity（attention head pruning + KV cache eviction）的 severity 不是固定常数——它是 eviction strategy 和 reasoning phase 交互的涌现属性。Importance-based eviction 的 compound interaction severity（paired median difference: median(II_low_i − II_high_i)）随 sparsity 单调递增（DeepSeek: 0.32→1.15→3.97→9.94; Qwen: 0.048→0.208→1.109→2.619），而 random masking 三相 II 持平（7/8 FAIL; Qwen s030 PASS p=0.012 but d=-0.04 negligible）。Cohen's d（paired d_z, B=10000）从 small-medium 到 large（DeepSeek: 0.44→0.80; Qwen: 0.28→1.02，s015 边缘）。这一发现挑战了 compound degradation 均匀分布的隐含假设。\\n\\n**数字基准**：所有数字基于 bf16 per-problem paired 统计量（B=10000 bootstrap）。median_diff 定义为 median(II_low_i − II_high_i)（逐 problem 配对差的中位数）。fp16 旧数据已弃用。\\n\\n## Narrative Arc\\n\\nCompound sparsity 是高效 LLM 推理的实用部署需求，但 prior work（SparK, Ada-KV, SLiM）将 compound interaction 视为均匀超可加。我们发现 severity 是 strategy × phase 交互的涌现属性：importance eviction 的 compound interaction 在 low-sparsity phase 远大于 high-sparsity phase（paired median difference 单调递增 0.32→9.94），而 random eviction 在所有 phase 保持平坦（7/8 FAIL; 唯一例外 Qwen s030 p=0.012 效应量可忽略 d=-0.04）。Cross-model replication 方向一致（Qwen magnitude ~1/4 of DeepSeek，onset threshold s≥0.20）。C3 crossover 现象的 LLaMA 架构验证已列入计划（plan_012 pilot-first），以测试发现的跨架构泛化性。我们通过 discrimination collapse 机制解释这一现象：head pruning → importance score 的判别力崩塌（Gini/CV 下降跨模型一致）→ importance eviction 退化为有偏伪随机。Attention entropy redistribution 作为 model-dependent secondary effect 在 DeepSeek 上观察到，但在 Qwen 上不稳健（D044）。\\n\\n**Framing 硬约束（D015）**：\\n- MUST: \\\\\\\"emergent phase-strategy interaction\\\\\\\"\\n- MUST NOT: \\\\\\\"importance metric limitation / importance is broken\\\\\\\"\\n- MUST NOT: claim \\\\\\\"importance masking produces super-additive interaction\\\\\\\" without qualifier — random masking also produces positive II. The distinguishing signal is **phase-dependent gradient** (importance: paired median difference 0.32→9.94 单调递增 vs random: 7/8 FAIL), not the existence of super-additivity itself (D023)\\n\\n## Core Claims\\n\\n| ID | Claim | Status | Key Evidence |\\n|----|-------|--------|-------------|\\n| C1 | Compound sparsity severity is strategy-dependent | confirmed | importance paired median diff 0.32→9.94 单调递增 vs random 7/8 FAIL (bf16; Qwen s030 PASS p=0.012 d=-0.04 negligible) |\\n| C2 | Severity is phase-modulated (amplified in low-sparsity phase) | confirmed | bf16 paired median difference 单调递增: 0.32→1.15→3.97→9.94; Bonferroni p<0.005 全 4 水平 (D032)。预注册：n=500 后若 QW s015 CI 仍跨零，叙事改为 'compound effect emerges above threshold sparsity (~s020)' |\\n| C3 | Phase-strategy crossover exists | confirmed | s=0.25: importance high_phase=0.032 < random=0.818, importance low_phase=7.196 > random=1.363 (bf16) |\\n| C4 | Discrimination collapse as primary mechanism; entropy redistribution model-dependent | partially_confirmed | D044: Gini/CV 下降跨模型一致; entropy collapse DeepSeek 一致但 Qwen s025 反转(+0.045) s030≈0。30/30 problems completed. |\\n| C5 | Phase-modulated compound severity generalizes across architectures | pilot_pending | Pilot-first: 2h oracle + heuristic (token position ratio as phase proxy) on LLaMA-8B, s025/s030. Gate: recover >30% compound degradation → full rollout; else → future work. Must include heuristic baseline (oracle upper bound + simple heuristic lower bound). |\\n| C5b | Phase-aware mitigation recovers compound degradation | planned | pilot: oracle + heuristic (token position ratio) on s025/s030, >30% recovery threshold |\\n| C6 | Severity gradient is sub-exponential | confirmed | Growth rate decreasing: Δ增长因子 3.8×→4.8×→2.0×（递减） |\\n| C7 | Statistical power at n=500 confirms dose-response | running | c2_n500_deepseek + c2_qw_n500 (n=500, 8 conditions each) |\\n| C8 | Cross-architecture validation (LLaMA) | planned | c3_llama (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B) |\\n| C9 | Differentiation from Lethe/SparK prior work | planned | Related Work §2 引用区分 + SparK gap analysis |\\n| S3→C | Onset threshold is model-dependent (DeepSeek s≥0.15, Qwen s≥0.20) | confirmed | D037/D038: Qwen s015 d=0.28 CI含0, s020+ 显著 |\\n| S7→C | Dose-response monotonicity (Spearman ρ=1.0) | confirmed | D047: 6-point dose-response, 单调递增 |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### RC1: Generalizability — 7B models only, single benchmark\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"Results on two 7B models and MATH-500 only. How do we know this generalizes to larger models or other reasoning tasks?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Cross-model replication (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B + Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) 控制架构变量、隔离训练方式影响（distillation vs instruction tuning）。注意：两模型共享 Qwen2ForCausalLM 架构，不能 claim 跨架构泛化。MATH-500 是 standard benchmark。Limitations 必须诚实声明：(1) 同架构不同训练，不是跨架构；(2) 7B scale only；(3) 单 benchmark。机制解释 (discrimination collapse) 原则上 architecture-agnostic，但未实证验证。\\n- **实验支撑**: C5 (Qwen cross-model). 若需加强可扩展到不同架构（如 LLaMA/Mistral），但 11 天 deadline 内不现实\\n\\n### RC2: Mechanism — entropy-discrimination collapse 缺乏直接测量\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"The mechanism is conceptual. Where is the empirical evidence for entropy increase and discrimination collapse?\\\"\\n- **应对**: c4_entropy 实验已完成（DeepSeek 30/30 + Qwen 30/30）。D044 结论：Gini/CV 下降跨模型一致（discrimination collapse confirmed）；entropy redistribution 在 DeepSeek 上一致但在 Qwen 上 s025 方向反转、s030 近零（model-dependent）。论文以 discrimination collapse 为主线，entropy 作 DeepSeek 特有的强化因素。\\n- **实验支撑**: c4_entropy (DeepSeek DONE) + qwen_c4_entropy (DONE, negative for entropy universality)\\n\\n### RC3: Practical impact — 没有 throughput 数据\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"No wall-clock or throughput measurements. Is the severity amplification reflected in actual inference speed?\\\"\\n- **应对**: 本文定位是 empirical finding paper（发现 + 机制解释），不是 method paper。Throughput 不是 claim 的一部分。Discussion 中明确 phase-gated controller 作为 future direction。\\n  - C5b pilot-first 策略：先 2h 在 s025/s030 上测 oracle upper bound + simple heuristic (token position ratio as phase proxy)。Oracle 回收 >30% compound degradation 才全面铺开，否则降为 future work\\n  - Heuristic baseline 必须包含：oracle 上界 + simple heuristic 下界，compute-matched\\n- **实验支撑**: 不需要额外实验（mitigation pilot 见 C5b / plan_011）\\n\\n### RC4: Statistical rigor — bootstrap 是否足够\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"Per-problem bootstrap with 50 problems — sufficient sample size?\\\"\\n- **应对**: bf16 使用 B=10000 per-problem paired bootstrap，有效配对数 n=49 (DeepSeek) / n=46 (Qwen)。Permutation test 使用 paired median difference 作为 robust statistic（D032），Bonferroni 校正后全 4 水平 p<0.005。Cohen's d（paired d_z）作为辅助效应量（DeepSeek 0.44→0.80, Qwen 0.28→1.02）。可在 appendix 补充 per-problem 分布图（已生成 fig4_per_problem_dist.pdf）。\\n- **实验支撑**: bf16 permutation test (D032) + Cohen's d per-problem paired (rebootstrap B=10000) 已完成\\n\\n### RC5: Novelty — prior work 是否已覆盖 phase-dependent sparsity\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"ThinKV and phase-aware methods already consider phase. What's new?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Prior work 在单轴上分析 phase（ThinKV: phase-aware eviction）或 compound（SparK: joint compression）。没有工作同时分析 compound × phase × strategy 的三维交互。Crossover 现象（同一 strategy 在不同 phase 方向反转）是全新发现。Related Work 加 Lethe 引用区分 + SparK 对比。\\n- **实验支撑**: C3 (crossover), Related Work §2 的 gap 分析\\n\\n### RC6: Novelty vs Lethe/SparK\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"How does this differ from Lethe (AAAI 2026) which also does layer-time-adaptive KV pruning, or SparK which studies compound compression?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Lethe 和 SparK 都没有分析 phase × strategy 的交互效应。Lethe 是 method paper（提出 adaptive eviction 方法），我们是 empirical finding paper（发现 phase-dependent severity gradient）。SparK 研究 compound compression 但将 interaction 视为均匀超可加，我们发现 interaction severity 随 phase 和 strategy 变化。Related Work §2 明确区分三者定位。\\n- **实验支撑**: 不需要额外实验，仅需 §2 引用区分\\n\\n## Paper Positioning\\n\\n**类型**: Empirical finding paper（非 method paper）\\n**核心贡献**:\\n1. 发现 compound sparsity severity 的 strategy × phase 交互\\n2. 量化 severity gradient（bf16 paired median difference 0.32→9.94, Cohen's d 0.44→0.80）和 crossover 现象\\n3. 提出 discrimination collapse 机制解释（Gini/CV 跨模型一致；entropy redistribution model-dependent）\\n4. Cross-model replication 验证泛化性\\n\\n**投稿目标**: EMNLP 2026 ARR, deadline 2026-05-25\\n**Rebuttal 准备**: Day 9 (2026-05-23) 预留 rebuttal letter 写作时间\\n**关键时间线**：\\n- Day 9 (2026-05-23): 预留写 rebuttal letter\\n- Day 11 (2026-05-25): EMNLP 2026 ARR 截稿\\n\", \"total_chars\": 8000, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

1	# PACS Profiling — full MVP pipeline (v4: importance-based masking).
2	#
3	# End-to-end:
4	#   1. Load causal LM + MATH-500 (stratified 50 problems)
5	#   2. Generate reasoning traces, segment into phases (quantile-based)
6	#   3. Per problem: dense forward with output_attentions to get CE losses
7	#      + head importance (Frobenius norm) + KV importance (cumulative attention)
8	#   4. For each sparsity level (10%, 20%):
9	#      - Build importance-based masks (prune least important heads/KV positions)
10	#      - Compute per-phase perplexity under 4 conditions (dense/attn/kv/both)
11	#      - Compute per-phase Interaction Index
12	#   5. Bootstrap 95% CI per (phase, level)
13	#   6. Pass/fail judgment (CI overlap + sign reversal, OR logic)
14	#   7. Output structured JSON
15	#
16	# Usage:
17	#   python -m pacs.profiling.run_profiling \
18	#     --model_path &lt;HF model ID or path> \
19	#     --data_path &lt;MATH-500 JSON> \
20	#     --output_dir ./profiling_output
21	
22	from __future__ import annotations
23	
24	import argparse
25	import json
26	import logging
27	import os
28	import sys
29	from collections import defaultdict
30	from itertools import combinations
31	from pathlib import Path
32	from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
33	
34	import numpy as np
35	import torch
36	
37	from ..utils.data_utils import stratified_sample_math500
38	from ..utils.metrics import compute_perplexity, ppl_from_cached_losses
39	from .interaction_index import InteractionIndexCalculator, _HeadMaskHook, bootstrap_interaction_index
40	from .phase_segmentation import PhaseSegmenter
41	from .alt_segmentation import segment_by_quantile, to_phase_annotation
42	
43	logging.basicConfig(
44	    level=logging.INFO,
45	    format="%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(name)s: %(message)s",
46	)
47	logger = logging.getLogger("run_profiling")
48	
49	DEFAULT_SPARSITY_LEVELS = [0.1, 0.2, 0.3]
50	N_BOOTSTRAP = 1000
51	MIN_SEGMENTS_WARN = 20
52	SEED = 42
53	
54	
55	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
56	# Model / data loading
57	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
58	
59	def load_model_and_tokenizer(model_path: str, device: str = "cuda"):
60	    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer
61	
62	    logger.info("Loading tokenizer from %s", model_path)
63	    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_path, trust_remote_code=True)
64	    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:
65	        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token
66	
67	    logger.info("Loading model from %s", model_path)
68	    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
69	        model_path,
70	        torch_dtype=torch.float16,
71	        device_map=device,
72	        trust_remote_code=True,
73	        attn_implementation="eager",
74	    )
75	    model.eval()
76	    return model, tokenizer
77	
78	
79	def load_data(data_path: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
80	    logger.info("Loading data from %s", data_path)
81	    with open(data_path) as f:
82	        data = json.load(f)
83	    if isinstance(data, dict) and "data" in data:
84	        data = data["data"]
85	    logger.info("Loaded %d problems", len(data))
86	    return data
87	
88	
89	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
90	# Phase-conditioned perplexity
91	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
92	
93	def compute_conditioned_perplexities(
94	    ii_calc: InteractionIndexCalculator,
95	    input_ids: torch.Tensor,
96	    phase_token_indices: List[int],
97	    attn_head_mask: torch.Tensor,
98	    kv_mask: torch.Tensor,
99	    dense_ce_losses: torch.Tensor,
100	    seq_len: int,
101	) -> Dict[str, float]:
102	    """Run 3 sparse forward passes; dense ppl from cached CE losses."""
103	    ppl_dense = ppl_from_cached_losses(dense_ce_losses, phase_token_indices, seq_len)
104	
105	    ppl_attn = ii_calc.forward_with_sparse_config(
106	        input_ids, attn_head_mask=attn_head_mask,
107	        phase_token_indices=phase_token_indices,
108	    )
109	    ppl_kv = ii_calc.forward_with_sparse_config(
110	        input_ids, kv_mask=kv_mask,
111	        phase_token_indices=phase_token_indices,
112	    )
113	    ppl_both = ii_calc.forward_with_sparse_config(
114	        input_ids, attn_head_mask=attn_head_mask, kv_mask=kv_mask,
115	        phase_token_indices=phase_token_indices,
116	    )
117	
118	    return {"dense": ppl_dense, "attn": ppl_attn, "kv": ppl_kv, "both": ppl_both}
119	
120	
121	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
122	# Pass/fail judgment (OR logic)
123	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
124	
125	def evaluate_pass_fail(
126	    ii_results: Dict[str, Dict[str, Dict[str, float]]],
127	) -> Dict[str, Any]:
128	    """Check pass criteria across phases.
129	
130	    Args:
131	        ii_results: {phase_name: {level_str: {"mean", "ci_low", "ci_high"}}}.
132	
133	    Returns:
134	        Dict with ci_overlap_test, sign_reversal_test, overall verdict.
135	    """
136	    phase_names = list(ii_results.keys())
137	    ci_pass = False
138	    ci_detail = ""
139	    sign_pass = False
140	    sign_detail = ""
141	
142	    all_levels = set()
143	    for pdata in ii_results.values():
144	        all_levels.update(pdata.keys())
145	
146	    for level_str in sorted(all_levels):
147	        phase_cis = {}
148	        for pname in phase_names:
149	            entry = ii_results.get(pname, {}).get(level_str)
150	            if entry is None:
151	                continue
152	            phase_cis[pname] = (entry["ci_low"], entry["ci_high"], entry["mean"])
153	
154	        for (p1, c1), (p2, c2) in combinations(phase_cis.items(), 2):
155	            # NaN guard: skip pairs with non-finite CI bounds
156	            if not (np.isfinite(c1[0]) and np.isfinite(c1[1]) and np.isfinite(c2[0]) and np.isfinite(c2[1])):
157	                continue
158	            # CI non-overlap
159	            if c1[1] < c2[0] or c2[1] < c1[0]:
160	                ci_pass = True
161	                ci_detail = f"{p1} vs {p2} CI non-overlapping at {level_str}"
162	            # Sign reversal: one strictly positive, other strictly negative
163	            sign1 = "pos" if c1[0] > 0 else ("neg" if c1[1] < 0 else "zero")
164	            sign2 = "pos" if c2[0] > 0 else ("neg" if c2[1] < 0 else "zero")
165	            if {sign1, sign2} == {"pos", "neg"}:
166	                sign_pass = True
167	                sign_detail = f"{p1} II{'>' if sign1 == 'pos' else '<'}0, {p2} II{'>' if sign2 == 'pos' else '<'}0 at {level_str}"
168	
169	    overall = "PASS" if (ci_pass or sign_pass) else "FAIL"
170	    return {
171	        "ci_overlap_test": {"pass": ci_pass, "details": ci_detail},
172	        "sign_reversal_test": {"pass": sign_pass, "details": sign_detail},
173	        "overall": overall,
174	    }
175	
176	
177	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
178	# Main pipeline
179	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
180	
181	@torch.no_grad()
182	def run_profiling(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
183	    device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
184	    torch.manual_seed(SEED)
185	    sparsity_levels = args.sparsity_levels
186	    use_importance = (args.masking_method == "importance")
187	    model, tokenizer = load_model_and_tokenizer(args.model_path, device=device)
188	
189	    raw_data = load_data(args.data_path)
190	    data = stratified_sample_math500(raw_data, seed=SEED)
191	    logger.info("Stratified sample: %d problems", len(data))
192	
193	    segmenter = PhaseSegmenter(
194	        bandwidth=args.kde_bandwidth,
195	        min_phase_length=args.min_phase_length,
196	    )
197	    ii_calc = InteractionIndexCalculator(model, tokenizer, device=device)
198	
199	    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
200	    warnings_list: List[str] = []
201	
202	    # Accumulators: {phase_name: {level_str: [ii_values]}}
203	    ii_per_phase_level: Dict[str, Dict[str, List[float]]] = defaultdict(
204	        lambda: defaultdict(list)
205	    )
206	    # Accumulators: {phase_name: {level_str: {condition: [ppl_values]}}}
207	    ppl_per_phase_level: Dict[str, Dict[str, Dict[str, List[float]]]] = defaultdict(
208	        lambda: defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
209	    )
210	    # Phase token counts
211	    phase_token_counts: Dict[str, List[int]] = defaultdict(list)
212	    phase_segment_counts: Dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
213	
214	    # P1 diagnostics accumulators
215	    all_problem_diagnostics: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
216	    all_sparsity_values: List[float] = []
217	
218	    # Locate attention layers once for hook registration (C1/C3 fix)
219	    attn_layers = _HeadMaskHook._find_attention_layers(model)
220	    if not attn_layers:
221	        raise RuntimeError("Could not find attention layers in model")
222	    last_attn_layer = attn_layers[-1]
223	
224	    for idx, item in enumerate(data):
225	        problem_text = item.get("problem", item.get("question", ""))
226	        problem_id = item.get("id", str(idx))
227	        logger.info("[%d/%d] Processing problem %s", idx + 1, len(data), problem_id)
228	
229	        prompt = (
230	            f"Solve the following math problem step by step.\n\n"
231	            f"Problem: {problem_text}\n\nSolution:"
232	        )
233	        inputs = tokenizer(prompt, return_tensors="pt").to(device)
234	        prompt_len = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]
235	
236	        # --- C1/C3 fix: hook-based per-step sparsity, no output_attentions accumulation ---
237	        step_sparsities: List[float] = []
238	
239	        def _enable_attn_pre_hook(module, args, kwargs):
240	            kwargs = dict(kwargs)
241	            kwargs["output_attentions"] = True
242	            return args, kwargs
243	
244	        def _capture_sparsity_hook(module, input, output):
245	            if isinstance(output, tuple) and len(output) > 1 and output[1] is not None:
246	                attn_w = output[1]
247	                if attn_w.shape[-2] == 1:
248	                    step_sparsities.append(
249	                        segmenter.compute_step_sparsity(attn_w[0])
250	                    )
251	                return (output[0],) + (None,) + output[2:]
252	
253	        pre_h = last_attn_layer.register_forward_pre_hook(
254	            _enable_attn_pre_hook, with_kwargs=True
255	        )
256	        post_h = last_attn_layer.register_forward_hook(_capture_sparsity_hook)
257	
258	        try:
259	            outputs = model.generate(
260	                **inputs,
261	                max_new_tokens=args.max_new_tokens,
262	                do_sample=False,
263	            )
264	        finally:
265	            pre_h.remove()
266	            post_h.remove()
267	
268	        full_ids = outputs.sequences if hasattr(outputs, "sequences") else outputs
269	        if full_ids.dim() == 1:
270	            full_ids = full_ids.unsqueeze(0)
271	
272	        if len(step_sparsities) == 0:
273	            logger.warning("No generation steps for problem %s, skipping.", problem_id)
274	            warnings_list.append(f"No generation steps for {problem_id}")
275	            continue
276	
277	        # Phase segmentation on the generation trace (quantile-based)
278	        gen_sparsity = np.array(step_sparsities)
279	        quant_seg = segment_by_quantile(gen_sparsity, n_groups=3)
280	        annotation = to_phase_annotation(
281	            quant_seg.assignments,
282	            quant_seg.group_names,
283	            gen_sparsity,
284	            offset=0,
285	            min_phase_length=args.min_phase_length,
286	        )
287	
288	        # P1: collect per-problem sparsity diagnostics
289	        problem_diagnostics = {
290	            "problem_id": problem_id,
291	            "sparsity_values": [float(s) for s in step_sparsities],
292	            "n_tokens": len(step_sparsities),
293	            "sparsity_mean": float(np.mean(gen_sparsity)),
294	            "sparsity_std": float(np.std(gen_sparsity)),
295	            "sparsity_min": float(np.min(gen_sparsity)),
296	            "sparsity_max": float(np.max(gen_sparsity)),
297	            "n_phases_detected": len(annotation.phases),
298	            "phase_sizes": [p.end - p.start for p in annotation.phases],
299	            "phase_names": [p.name for p in annotation.phases],
300	            "segmentation_method": "quantile",
301	            "quantile_boundaries": quant_seg.boundaries.tolist(),
302	            "quantile_group_stats": quant_seg.group_stats,
303	        }
304	        all_problem_diagnostics.append(problem_diagnostics)
305	        all_sparsity_values.extend(problem_diagnostics["sparsity_values"])
306	
307	        # Offset phase indices to full_ids coordinates (prompt + generation)
308	        for phase in annotation.phases:
309	            phase.start += prompt_len
310	            phase.end += prompt_len
311	
312	        # --- Dense forward: importance-based extracts head/KV importance; random skips it ---
313	        if use_importance:
314	            dense_ce_losses, head_importance, kv_importance = (
315	                ii_calc.compute_dense_forward_with_importance(full_ids)
316	            )
317	        else:
318	            dense_ce_losses = ii_calc.compute_dense_ce_losses(full_ids)
319	            head_importance = None
320	            kv_importance = None
321	        full_seq_len = full_ids.shape[1]
322	
323	        for phase in annotation.phases:
324	            phase_token_counts[phase.name].append(len(phase.token_indices))
325	            phase_segment_counts[phase.name] += 1
326	
327	        # --- C2 fix: generate masks once per level, reuse across phases ---
328	        num_layers, num_heads = ii_calc._get_model_dims()
329	
330	        for level in sparsity_levels:
331	            level_str = str(level)
332	            attn_head_mask = InteractionIndexCalculator._select_heads_to_mask(
333	                num_layers, num_heads, level,
334	                importance=head_importance,
335	            )
336	            kv_mask = InteractionIndexCalculator._select_kv_to_evict(
337	                full_seq_len, level,
338	                importance=kv_importance,
339	            )
340	
341	            for phase in annotation.phases:
342	                ppls = compute_conditioned_perplexities(
343	                    ii_calc, full_ids, phase.token_indices,
344	                    attn_head_mask, kv_mask,
345	                    dense_ce_losses, full_seq_len,
346	                )
347	                for cond, val in ppls.items():
348	                    ppl_per_phase_level[phase.name][level_str][cond].append(val)
349	
350	                ppl_d = ppls["dense"]
351	                if ppl_d > 0:
352	                    r_attn = ppls["attn"] / ppl_d
353	                    r_kv = ppls["kv"] / ppl_d
354	                    r_both = ppls["both"] / ppl_d
355	                    ii_val = r_both - r_attn - r_kv + 1
356	                else:
357	                    ii_val = float("nan")
358	                ii_per_phase_level[phase.name][level_str].append(ii_val)
359	
360	        logger.info(
361	            "  -> %d phases, boundaries at %s",
362	            len(annotation.phases), annotation.boundaries,
363	        )
364	
365	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
366	    # Aggregate: per-phase stats
367	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
368	    phase_stats: Dict[str, Any] = {}
369	    for pname in sorted(phase_token_counts.keys()):
370	        n_tokens = sum(phase_token_counts[pname])
371	        n_segments = phase_segment_counts[pname]
372	        phase_stats[pname] = {"n_tokens": n_tokens, "n_segments": n_segments}
373	        if n_segments < MIN_SEGMENTS_WARN:
374	            msg = f"Phase '{pname}' has only {n_segments} segments (< {MIN_SEGMENTS_WARN})"
375	            warnings_list.append(msg)
376	            logger.warning(msg)
377	
378	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
379	    # Aggregate: mean perplexity per (phase, level, condition)
380	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
381	    perplexity_out: Dict[str, Dict[str, Dict[str, float]]] = {}
382	    for pname in sorted(ppl_per_phase_level.keys()):
383	        perplexity_out[pname] = {}
384	        for level_str in sorted(ppl_per_phase_level[pname].keys()):
385	            cond_means: Dict[str, float] = {}
386	            for cond in ["dense", "attn", "kv", "both"]:
387	                vals = ppl_per_phase_level[pname][level_str][cond]
388	                valid = [v for v in vals if not (isinstance(v, float) and v != v)]
389	                cond_means[cond] = round(float(np.mean(valid)), 4) if valid else float("nan")
390	            perplexity_out[pname][level_str] = cond_means
391	
392	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
393	    # Bootstrap CI per (phase, level)
394	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
395	    ii_out: Dict[str, Dict[str, Dict[str, float]]] = {}
396	    for pname in sorted(ii_per_phase_level.keys()):
397	        ii_out[pname] = {}
398	        for level_str in sorted(ii_per_phase_level[pname].keys()):
399	            samples = [v for v in ii_per_phase_level[pname][level_str] if not np.isnan(v)]
400	            if len(samples) < 2:
401	                ii_out[pname][level_str] = {
402	                    "mean": float(np.mean(samples)) if samples else float("nan"),
403	                    "ci_low": float("nan"),
404	                    "ci_high": float("nan"),
405	                }
406	                warnings_list.append(
407	                    f"Phase '{pname}' at {level_str}: only {len(samples)} valid II samples, CI unavailable"
408	                )
409	            else:

411	                    samples, n_resamples=N_BOOTSTRAP, seed=SEED,
412	                )

414	                    "mean": round(mean, 6),
415	                    "ci_low": round(ci_low, 6),
416	                    "ci_high": round(ci_high, 6),
417	                }

444	            "kv_importance_metric": "cumulative_attention_received" if use_importance else "none",

473	            print(

529	    else:

531	    return args
532

load_model_and_tokenizer(model_path: str, device: str = \"cuda\"):\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\n\n    logger.info(\"Loading tokenizer from %s\", model_path)\n    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_path, trust_remote_code=True)\n    if tokenizer.pad_token is None:\n        tokenizer.pad_token = tokenizer.eos_token\n\n    logger.info(\"Loading model from %s\", model_path)\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        model_path,\n        torch_dtype=torch.float16,\n        device_map=device,\n        trust_remote_code=True,\n        attn_implementation=\"eager\",\n    )\n    model.eval()\n    return model, tokenizer\n\n\ndef load_data(data_path: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n    logger.info(\"Loading data from %s\", data_path)\n return data\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Phase-conditioned perplexity\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef compute_conditioned_perplexities(\n    ii_calc: InteractionIndexCalculator,\n    input_ids: torch.Tensor,\n    phase_token_indices: List[int],\n    attn_head_mask: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_mask: torch.Tensor,\n    dense_ce_losses: torch.Tensor,\n    seq_len: int,\n) -> Dict[str, float]:\n    \"\"\"Run 3 sparse forward passes; dense ppl from cached CE losses.\"\"\"\n    ppl_dense = ppl_from_cached_losses(dense_ce_losses, phase_token_indices, seq_len)\n\n    ppl_attn = ii_calc.forward_with_sparse_config(\n phase_token_indices=phase_token_indices,\n    )\n    ppl_both = ii_calc.forward_with_sparse_config(\n        input_ids, attn_head_mask=attn_head_mask, kv_mask=kv_mask,\n        phase_token_indices=phase_token_indices,\n    )\n\n    return {\"dense\": ppl_dense, \"attn\": ppl_attn, \"kv\": ppl_kv, \"both\": ppl_both}\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Pass/fail judgment (OR logic)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef evaluate_pass_fail(\n    ii_results: Dict[str, Dict[str, Dict[str, float]]],\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Check pass criteria across phases.\n\n    Args:\n        ii_results: {phase_name: {level_str: {\"mean\", \"ci_low\", \"ci_high\"}}}.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict with ci_overlap_test, sign_reversal_test, overall verdict.\n    \"\"\"\n    phase_names = list(ii_results.keys())\n    ci_pass = False\n    ci_detail = \"\"\n    sign_pass = False\n    sign_detail = \"\"\n\n    all_levels = set()\n    for pdata in ii_results.values():\n        all_levels.update(pdata.keys())\n\n    for level_str in sorted(all_levels):\n        phase_cis = {}\n        for pname in phase_names:\n            entry = ii_results.get(pname, {}).get(level_str)\n            if entry is None:\n                continue\n            phase_cis[pname] = (entry[\"ci_low\"], entry[\"ci_high\"], entry[\"mean\"])\n\n        for (p1, c1), (p2, c2) in combinations(phase_cis.items(), 2):\n            # NaN guard: skip pairs with non-finite CI bounds\n            if not (np.isfinite(c1[0]) and np.isfinite(c1[1]) and np.isfinite(c2[0]) and np.isfinite(c2[1])):\n                continue\n            # CI non-overlap\n            if c1[1] < c2[0] or c2[1] < c1[0]:\n                ci_pass = True\n                ci_detail = f\"{p1} vs {p2} CI non-overlapping at {level_str}\"\n            # Sign reversal: one strictly positive, other strictly negative\n            sign1 = \"pos\" if c1[0] > 0 else (\"neg\" if c1[1] < 0 else \"zero\")\n            sign2 = \"pos\" if c2[0] > 0 else (\"neg\" if c2[1] < 0 else \"zero\")\n            if {sign1, sign2} == {\"pos\", \"neg\"}:\n                sign_pass = True\n                sign_detail = f\"{p1} II{'>' if sign1 == 'pos' else '<'}0, {p2} II{'>' if sign2 == 'pos' else '<'}0 at {level_str}\"\n\n    overall = \"PASS\" if (ci_pass or sign_pass) else \"FAIL\"\n    return {\n        \"ci_overlap_test\": {\"pass\": ci_pass, \"details\": ci_detail},\n        \"sign_reversal_test\": {\"pass\": sign_pass, \"details\": sign_detail},\n        \"overall\": overall,\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Main pipeline\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n@torch.no_grad()\ndef {problem_text}\\n\\nSolution:\"\n        )\n        inputs = tokenizer(prompt, return_tensors=\"pt\").to(device)\n        prompt_len = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]\n\n        # --- C1/C3 fix: full_ids = outputs.sequences if hasattr(outputs, \"sequences\") else outputs\n        if full_ids.dim() == 1:\n            full_ids = full_ids.unsqueeze(0)\n\n        if len(step_sparsities) == 0:\n {stats['n_segments']} segments\")\n    print()\n    for pname in sorted(ii_out.keys()):\n        for level_str in sorted(ii_out[pname].keys()):\n            e = ii_out[pname][level_str]\n            print(\n                f\"  II[{pname}, {level_str}]: \"\n                f\"mean={e['mean']:+.4f}  95%CI=[{e['ci_low']:+.4f}, {e['ci_high']:+.4f}]\"\n            )\n    print(f\"\\nVerdict: 1	# Interaction Index (II) calculator for measuring super/sub-additivity
2	# of compound sparsity (attention head pruning + KV cache eviction).
3	#
4	# II = R_both - R_attn - R_kv + 1
5	# where R_x = ppl_x / ppl_dense (relative degradation ratio).
6	# II > 0 → super-additive degradation (compound sparsity hurts more than sum of parts)
7	# II < 0 → sub-additive degradation
8	# II = 0 → independent
9	
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	import math
13	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
14	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
15	
16	import numpy as np
17	
18	import torch
19	import torch.nn.functional as F
20	
21	from ..utils.metrics import compute_perplexity
22	
23	try:
24	    from .phase_segmentation import Phase
25	except ImportError:
26	    Phase = None  # will be resolved when phase_segmentation is created
27	
28	
29	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
30	# Data classes
31	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
32	
33	@dataclass
34	class InteractionResult:
35	    ii_value: float
36	    ppl_dense: float
37	    ppl_attn_sparse: float
38	    ppl_kv_sparse: float
39	    ppl_both_sparse: float
40	    r_attn: float          # ppl_attn / ppl_dense
41	    r_kv: float            # ppl_kv / ppl_dense
42	    r_both: float          # ppl_both / ppl_dense
43	    is_super_additive: bool  # II > 0 → compound sparsity degrades more than sum of parts
44	    sparsity_level: float = 0.0
45	    phase_name: Optional[str] = None
46	    num_tokens: int = 0
47	
48	    def summary(self) -> str:
49	        tag = "super-additive" if self.is_super_additive else "sub-additive"
50	        return (
51	            f"II={self.ii_value:+.4f} ({tag})  "
52	            f"R_attn={self.r_attn:.4f}  R_kv={self.r_kv:.4f}  R_both={self.r_both:.4f}  "
53	            f"dense={self.ppl_dense:.2f}  attn={self.ppl_attn_sparse:.2f}  "
54	            f"kv={self.ppl_kv_sparse:.2f}  both={self.ppl_both_sparse:.2f}"
55	        )
56	
57	
58	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
59	# Hook-based head masking (works with GQA / MHA / MQA)
60	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
61	
62	class _HeadMaskHook:
63	    """Registers forward hooks that zero out masked attention heads."""
64	
65	    def __init__(self, model: torch.nn.Module, head_mask: torch.Tensor):
66	        """
67	        head_mask: (num_layers, num_heads) float tensor, 1 = active, 0 = masked.
68	        """
69	        self.handles: list = []
70	        attn_layers = self._find_attention_layers(model)
71	        for layer_idx, attn_module in enumerate(attn_layers):
72	            if layer_idx >= head_mask.size(0):
73	                break
74	            mask = head_mask[layer_idx]  # (num_heads,)
75	            handle = attn_module.register_forward_hook(
76	                self._make_hook(mask)
77	            )
78	            self.handles.append(handle)
79	
80	    @staticmethod

```text
1	# Compound sparse forward: head masking + KV eviction in a single pass.
2	#
3	# Head masking: forward hooks on attention output, zero out masked heads
4	# by reshaping to (bsz, seq, num_q_heads, head_dim) and scaling.
5	#
6	# KV eviction: fold the KV position mask into the attention_mask so
7	# evicted positions are invisible to all queries.
8	#
9	# Designed for attn_implementation="eager" — needs access to attention
10	# weights for importance computation. Triton version will come later.
11	#
12	# Key deps: torch, pacs.compound.{head_selector, kv_evictor}, pacs.utils.metrics
13	
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	from typing import List, Optional
17	
18	import torch
19	
20	from .head_selector import HeadMask
21	from .kv_evictor import KVMask
22	from ..utils.metrics import compute_perplexity
23	
24	
25	class CompoundSparseForward:
26	    """Execute a forward pass with simultaneous head masking and KV eviction."""
27	
28	    def __init__(self, model: torch.nn.Module, device: str = "cuda"):
29	        self.model = model
30	        self.device = device
31	
32	    @torch.no_grad()
33	    def forward(
34	        self,
35	        input_ids: torch.Tensor,
36	        head_mask: Optional[HeadMask] = None,
37	        kv_mask: Optional[KVMask] = None,
38	        attention_mask: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None,
39	    ) -> torch.Tensor:
40	        """Run forward with compound sparsity applied.
41	
42	        Returns:
43	            logits ``(batch, seq_len, vocab_size)``.  Evicted KV positions
44	            still produce logits (attention just cannot attend to them);
45	            downstream PPL computation should filter by ``token_indices``.
46	        """
47	        input_ids = input_ids.to(self.device)
48	        if attention_mask is None:
49	            attention_mask = torch.ones_like(input_ids)
50	        attention_mask = attention_mask.to(self.device)
51	
52	        # --- KV eviction: fold position mask into attention_mask ---
53	        if kv_mask is not None:
54	            kv_float = kv_mask.position_mask.to(self.device).float()
55	            attention_mask = attention_mask * kv_float.unsqueeze(0)
56	
57	        # --- Head masking: register output hooks on every attention layer ---
58	        handles: list = []
59	        if head_mask is not None:
60	            float_mask = head_mask.q_head_mask.float().to(self.device)
61	            for attn_module in self._find_attention_layers():
62	                handle = attn_module.register_forward_hook(
63	                    self._make_head_mask_hook(float_mask)
64	                )
65	                handles.append(handle)
66	
67	        try:
68	            outputs = self.model(
69	                input_ids=input_ids,
70	                attention_mask=attention_mask,
71	            )
72	        finally:
73	            for h in handles:
74	                h.remove()
75	
76	        return outputs.logits
77	
78	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
79	    # Internals
80	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
```

      "content": "# Compound sparse forward: head masking + KV eviction in a single pass.\n#\n# Head masking: forward hooks on attention output, zero out masked heads\n# by reshaping to (bsz, seq, num_q_heads, head_dim) and scaling.\n#\n# KV eviction: fold the KV position mask into the attention_mask so\n# evicted positions are invisible to all queries.\n#\n# Designed for attn_implementation=\"eager\" — needs access to attention\n# weights for importance computation. Triton version will come later.\n#\n# Key deps: torch, pacs.compound.{head_selector, kv_evictor}, pacs.utils.metrics\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import List, Optional\n\nimport torch\n\nfrom .head_selector import HeadMask\nfrom .kv_evictor import KVMask\nfrom ..utils.metrics import compute_perplexity\n\n\nclass CompoundSparseForward:\n    \"\"\"Execute a forward pass with simultaneous head masking and KV eviction.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, model: torch.nn.Module, device: str = \"cuda\"):\n        self.model = model\n        self.device = device\n\n    @torch.no_grad()\n    def forward(\n        self,\n        input_ids: torch.Tensor,\n        head_mask: Optional[HeadMask] = None,\n        kv_mask: Optional[KVMask] = None,\n        attention_mask: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None,\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        \"\"\"Run forward with compound sparsity applied.\n\n        Returns:\n            logits ``(batch, seq_len, vocab_size)``.  Evicted KV positions\n            still produce logits (attention just cannot attend to them);\n            downstream PPL computation should filter by ``token_indices``.\n        \"\"\"\n        input_ids = input_ids.to(self.device)\n        if attention_mask is None:\n            attention_mask = torch.ones_like(input_ids)\n        attention_mask = attention_mask.to(self.device)\n\n        # --- KV eviction: fold position mask into attention_mask ---\n        if kv_mask is not None:\n            kv_float = kv_mask.position_mask.to(self.device).float()\n            attention_mask = attention_mask * kv_float.unsqueeze(0)\n\n        # --- Head masking: register output hooks on every attention layer ---\n        handles: list = []\n        if head_mask is not None:\n            float_mask = head_mask.q_head_mask.float().to(self.device)\n            for attn_module in self._find_attention_layers():\n                handle = attn_module.register_forward_hook(\n                    self._make_head_mask_hook(float_mask)\n                )\n                handles.append(handle)\n\n        try:\n            outputs = self.model(\n                input_ids=input_ids,\n                attention_mask=attention_mask,\n            )\n        finally:\n            for h in handles:\n                h.remove()\n\n        return outputs.logits\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------",

1	"""
2	Phase Segmentation via Attention Sparsity Profiling.
3	
4	Implements the ThinKV (ICLR 2026 Oral) methodology:
5	  attention sparsity -> Gaussian KDE -> tri-modal phase detection.
6	
7	Each token is assigned to one of three phases:
8	  - execution:   low sparsity (dense attention), focused computation
9	  - reasoning:   intermediate sparsity, broad cross-token reasoning
10	  - transition:  high sparsity, uncertainty/backtracking patterns
11	"""
12	
13	from __future__ import annotations
14	
15	import logging
16	import warnings
17	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
18	from typing import List, Optional, Tuple
19	
20	import numpy as np
21	import torch
22	from scipy.signal import argrelmin
23	from scipy.stats import gaussian_kde
24	
25	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
26	
27	PHASE_NAMES_BY_SPARSITY = ["execution", "reasoning", "transition"]
28	
29	
30	@dataclass
31	class Phase:
32	    name: str            # "reasoning" / "execution" / "transition"
33	    start: int           # token start position (inclusive)
34	    end: int             # token end position (exclusive)
35	    mean_sparsity: float
36	
37	    @property
38	    def token_indices(self) -> List[int]:
39	        return list(range(self.start, self.end))
40	
41	
42	@dataclass
43	class PhaseAnnotation:
44	    phases: List[Phase]
45	    boundaries: List[int]       # token indices where phase transitions occur
46	    sparsity: np.ndarray        # raw per-token sparsity, shape (seq_len,)
47	    kde_x: np.ndarray
48	    kde_density: np.ndarray
49	    metadata: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
50	
51	
52	class PhaseSegmenter:
53	    """Segment a token sequence into reasoning / transition / execution phases
54	    based on the attention sparsity profile and Gaussian KDE peak detection."""
55	
56	    def __init__(
57	        self,
58	        bandwidth: str = "scott",
59	        min_phase_length: int = 10,
60	        sparsity_threshold_ratio: float = 0.01,

      "content": "\"\"\"\nPhase Segmentation via Attention Sparsity Profiling.\n\nImplements the ThinKV (ICLR 2026 Oral) methodology:\n  attention sparsity -> Gaussian KDE -> tri-modal phase detection.\n\nEach token is assigned to one of three phases:\n  - execution:   low sparsity (dense attention), focused computation\n  - reasoning:   intermediate sparsity, broad cross-token reasoning\n  - transition:  high sparsity, uncertainty/backtracking patterns\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nimport warnings\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Tuple\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom scipy.signal import argrelmin\nfrom scipy.stats import gaussian_kde\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nPHASE_NAMES_BY_SPARSITY = [\"execution\", \"reasoning\", \"transition\"]\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Phase:\n    name: str            # \"reasoning\" / \"execution\" / \"transition\"\n    start: int           # token start position (inclusive)\n    end: int             # token end position (exclusive)\n    mean_sparsity: float\n\n    @property\n    def token_indices(self) -> List[int]:\n        return list(range(self.start, self.end))\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass PhaseAnnotation:\n    phases: List[Phase]\n    boundaries: List[int]       # token indices where phase transitions occur\n    sparsity: np.ndarray        # raw per-token sparsity, shape (seq_len,)\n    kde_x: np.ndarray\n    kde_density: np.ndarray\n    metadata: dict = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n\nclass PhaseSegmenter:\n    \"\"\"Segment a token sequence into reasoning / transition / execution phases\n    based on the attention sparsity profile and Gaussian KDE peak detection.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        bandwidth: str = \"scott\",\n        min_phase_length: int = 10,\n        sparsity_threshold_ratio: float = 0.01,",
      "numLines": 60,

D005\\n- id: D007\\n  date: 2026-05-13 20:20 UTC\\n  summary: M1 结果无效（KV bug + KDE 失败）：两路并行——P0 修复重跑 + 替代 segmentation 开发\\n  rationale: KV eviction 的 per-layer mask 交集 bug 导致全 NaN（P0），KDE 82% 样本找不到 tri-modal\\n    结构（P1）。但积极信号存在：execution phase 对 head pruning 敏感度 474× vs reasoning 14×，说明 token\\n    级结构性差异真实存在。决策：(A) P0 修复后重跑 M1 获取有效 II + 诊断数据，(B) 并行开发 GMM+BIC / quantile-based\\n    / multi-layer 替代 segmentation。先看重跑 II 是否在现有 3 phase 间有差异，差异显著则 KDE 虽粗糙但假设成立；无差异则上替代方案。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_interaction_index_profiling\\n    role: failed_run\\n    result: KV NaN + KDE 82% fallback + 90% transition\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D001\\n- id: D008\\n  date: 2026-05-13 21:23 UTC\\n  summary: M1v2 假设未获支持但测量方法有问题——M1v3 最后诊断：降 sparsity + quantile segmentation + NaN\\n    guard\\n  rationale: M1v2 所有 phase 均 super-additive、无 sign reversal，但 3 个方法论问题使结论不可靠：(1) 30%\\n    random masking 导致 24-33× PPL 退化，超出安全区；(2) KDE 90% fallback，phase 划分无意义；(3) NaN\\n    CI artifact 导致虚假 PASS。M1v3 修复这三点后做最终判定。叙事 pivot：即使全 super-additive，magnitude 跨\\n    segment 差异（+17.8 vs +3.1）仍支持 phase-aware allocation。Pass = magnitude CI 不重叠；Fail\\n    = magnitude 无差异。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_ii_profiling_v2\\n    role: diagnostic\\n    result: All super-additive, no sign reversal, NaN artifact PASS, KDE 90% fallback\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D007\\n- id: D009\\n  date: 2026-05-13 22:44 UTC\\n  summary: Random masking 系统性混淆——v1-v3 测了错误条件；importance-based masking 是正确 MVP 测试，立即启动\\n    v4\\n  rationale: 三次 MVP 实验全用 random masking（torch.randperm 选 heads/KV），天然产生 super-additive\\n    interaction（随机移除组件造成相关性损伤）。初步 importance-vs-random 对比已显示 masking 方法是 II 符号的主导因子（importance\\n    II=-0.91 sub-additive vs random II=+9.38 super-additive）。v3 在 clean methodology\\n    下仍全 super-additive 是预期结果，不代表 PACS 假设无效。真正的 MVP 判定需在 importance-based masking 条件下测试\\n    per-phase II——这才是实际部署条件下的 compound sparsity 行为。v3 high vs mid 在 s=0.2 下 CI 几乎不重叠（[1.06,3.55]\\n    vs [-1.06,1.30]），暗示 importance-based 下 magnitude 差异可能更显著。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - D010\\n- id: D013\\n  date: 2026-05-14 01:16 UTC\\n  summary: 暂停 importance masking 结论，根因确认：cumulative_attention_received 有因果注意力位置偏差，驱逐最近上下文导致灾难性退化\\n  rationale: Reviewer 根因分析确认：causal attention 中 position 0 被 N-1 个 token 关注（累积最高），position\\n    N-1 仅被自身关注（累积最低）。topk(largest=False) 驱逐最低分 = 驱逐最新位置 = 破坏最近上下文。这解释了 importance\\n    eviction 比 random 差 26-60× 的现象。s025 的 II per-sample mean (+152.7) 与聚合计算 (-107.6)\\n    符号相反，CI 宽 455 单位，统计意义为零。D010 的 strategy-dependent direction reversal 可能完全是坏指标伪影。修复方向：position\\n    normalization importance[j] /= (seq_len - The key insight emerging is that importance-based pruning hits low-sparsity phases much harder than high-sparsity ones, so a phase-aware mitigation would need to allocate different sparsity budgets per phase.

For the oracle approach, I'd use the true phase labels from generation to selectively reduce sparsity during execution phases where compound interactions are severe, while increasing sparsity during transition phases where they're minimal, keeping total compute constant. The heuristic alternative would use token position as a cheap proxy for phase without needing actual attention weights, though this assumes early tokens correlate with reasoning and later tokens with concluding. So the experiment would compare three approaches: uniform sparsity at 0.25 across all tokens, an oracle version that uses lighter sparsity (0.15) during execution and heavier sparsity (0.35) during transitions while matching total compute, and a heuristic version using token position ratios instead of actual attention patterns. The success metric would be how much of the compound degradation we can recover.

For the pilot, I'm thinking around 20-30 problems with sparsity levels where the compound interaction is strongest (s=0.25-0.30), using DeepSeek since it shows clearer signals. This connects to the discrimination collapse story — when heads get pruned, importance scores lose their discriminative power, making importance-based eviction essentially random, which amplifies compound interaction.   "content": "# C5b Mitigation Pilot — Experiment Design\n\n> Claim: Phase-aware sparsity allocation recovers compound degradation\n> Status: plan_011 | Priority: P1 | Depends on: plan_009, plan_010 (n=500 running)\n> Gate: >30% II recovery → full rollout; else → future work\n> Budget: ~2h on 1 GPU (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Objective & Hypotheses\n\n**Goal**: Demonstrate that phase-aware sparsity budget allocation — informed by the phase-modulated severity gradient (C1/C2) — can reduce compound interaction degradation compared to uniform allocation.\n\n**Hypotheses**:\n- **H1 (Oracle)**: When true phase labels are known, reallocating sparsity budget away from the execution phase (high compound interaction) toward the transition phase (low compound interaction) recovers >30% of compound interaction (measured by II reduction).\n- **H2 (Heuristic)**: A zero-cost heuristic (token position ratio) recovers some compound interaction, but less than oracle, establishing a lower bound.\n\n**Why this matters**: C1-C3 establish that compound interaction is phase-dependent. C5b closes the loop: if severity is phase-dependent, phase-aware allocation should mitigate it. Even a modest pilot result (1) validates the practical relevance of the finding, and (2) provides a concrete pointer for future work on phase-gated controllers.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Pilot Configuration\n\n| Parameter | Value | Rationale |\n|-----------|-------|-----------|\n| Model | DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B | Strongest signal (median_diff 3.97/9.94 at s025/s030) |\n| n_problems | 30 | Sufficient for paired test (existing stratified sample subset) |\n| Sparsity levels | s=0.25, s=0.30 | Largest compound interaction, most room for recovery |\n| Phases | 3 (execution/reasoning/transition) | Quantile-based segmentation, consistent with C1-C3 |\n| Bootstrap | B=10000 | Consistent with bf16 canonical data |\n\n**Why not Qwen**: Qwen's compound interaction is ~1/4 of DeepSeek's magnitude (2.619 vs 9.94 at s030). Smaller signal → harder to detect recovery. If oracle recovers >30% on DeepSeek, Qwen replication is a separate follow-up.\n\n**Why not s015/s020**: Small compound interaction (0.32/1.15) leaves little room for meaningful recovery. Risk of floor effects.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Experimental Conditions (per problem, per sparsity level)\n\n### 3.1 Condition A — Uniform Baseline (existing data)\n\nStandard importance-based compound sparsity at level s, applied uniformly across all phases.\n\nThis is the **existing bf16 importance data** (already collected). No new GPU compute needed for this condition. We re-use per-problem II values from `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s025/` and `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s030/`.\n\n**Per-phase II_uniform(phase, s) from existing data**:\n- II(execution, s025) ≈ 7.20 (median), II(transition, s025) ≈ 0.053\n- II(execution, s030) ≈ 14.64 (median), II(transition, s030) ≈ 0.053\n\n### 3.2 Condition B — Oracle Phase-Aware Allocation\n\nPhase-dependent sparsity using **true quantile-based phase labels** (from attention sparsity during generation). This is an upper bound on mitigation performance — a real controller would have noisier phase estimates.\n\n**Allocation rule** (compute-matched):\n\nGiven target sparsity s and phases with token counts (n_exec, n_reas, n_trans):\n\n```\ns_exec  = s - δ           (reduce sparsity in high-interaction phase)\ns_reas  = s               (keep reasoning phase unchanged)\ns_trans = s + δ'           (increase sparsity in low-interaction phase)\n\nCompute match constraint:\n  n_exec × s_exec + n_reas × s_reas + n_trans × s_trans = N_total × s\n  → δ' = δ × (n_exec / n_trans)\n```\n\n**δ selection**: Use δ = 0.10 (i.e., execution phase gets s-0.10, transition gets s+δ').\n\n| Target s | s_exec | s_reas | s_trans (approx, depends on phase sizes) |\n|----------|--------|--------|------------------------------------------|\n| 0.25 | 0.15 | 0.25 | ~0.35 |\n| 0.30 | 0.20 | 0.30 | ~0.40 |\n\nNote: δ' is computed per-problem based on actual phase token counts to ensure exact compute match. s_trans is capped at 0.50 to avoid degenerate masking.\n\n**Implementation**: For each problem:\n1. Generate trace (reuse existing generation from bf16 runs, or regenerate if needed)\n2. Segment into phases (quantile-based, same as existing pipeline)\n3. For each phase, generate importance-based masks at s_phase\n4. Run compound forward with phase-specific masks, extract PPL on that phase's tokens\n5. Compute per-phase II under oracle allocation\n\n### 3.3 Condition C — Heuristic Baseline (Token Position Ratio)\n\nPhase assignment using **normalized generation position** as a zero-cost proxy:\n\n```\nphase_heuristic(token_pos) =\n  \"execution\"  if token_pos / seq_len < 0.33\n  \"reasoning\"  if 0.33 ≤ token_pos / seq_len < 0.67\n  \"transition\" if token_pos / seq_len ≥ 0.67\n```\n\nSame sparsity allocation rule as oracle, but applied based on positional phase labels. This is the simplest possible heuristic — it tests whether even crude position-based phase estimation captures enough signal for mitigation.\n\n**Why token position ratio**: (1) Zero compute cost — no attention weights needed. (2) Prior work (ThinKV) observes that reasoning models often follow a \"thinking → calculating → summarizing\" trajectory, correlating with token position. (3) Establishes a lower bound that any real phase detector must beat.\n\n**Alternative heuristic considered but deferred**: Cumulative attention entropy (requires attention weights, non-zero cost). If token position ratio shows >0% recovery, entropy-based heuristic becomes a natural follow-up.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Evaluation Metrics\n\n### 4.1 Primary: II Recovery Percentage\n\nFor each problem i, sparsity level s, and phase p:\n\n```\nII_uniform(i, s, p)  = II under Condition A\nII_oracle(i, s, p)   = II under Condition B\nII_heuristic(i, s, p) = II under Condition C\n```\n\n**Aggregate metric** (per sparsity level):\n\n```\nrecovery_oracle(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_oracle(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))\nrecovery_heuristic(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_heuristic(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))\n```\n\nWhere `II_weighted(i, s) = Σ_p [n_p × II(i, s, p)] / N_total` is the token-count-weighted mean II across phases for problem i.\n\n### 4.2 Secondary: Per-Phase II Comparison\n\nCompare per-phase II between uniform and oracle:\n- Execution phase: II should decrease (less sparsity → less compound interaction)\n- Transition phase: II should stay low (already near zero, even with more sparsity)\n- Net effect: overall weighted II decreases\n\n### 4.3 Gate Criterion\n\n```\nPASS if: recovery_oracle(s025) > 30% OR recovery_oracle(s030) > 30%\nFAIL otherwise → C5b = \"future work\"\n```\n\nIf heuristic also exceeds 30%, that's a bonus result but not required for the gate.\n\n### 4.4 Statistical Test\n\nPaired Wilcoxon signed-rank test on per-problem weighted II (uniform vs oracle, uniform vs heuristic). Report p-value and recovery percentage with bootstrap 95% CI (B=10000).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Implementation Plan\n\n### 5.1 Code Changes\n\nCreate `run_mitigation_pilot.py` extending the existing profiling pipeline:\n\n```\npacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py\n```\n\n**Key functions to add**:\n\n1. `compute_phase_aware_sparsity(target_s, delta, phase_token_counts) → Dict[str, float]`\n   - Returns per-phase sparsity levels, compute-matched\n   - Caps s_trans at 0.50\n\n2. `assign_heuristic_phases(seq_len, prompt_len) → List[Phase]`\n   - Token position ratio → phase assignment\n   - Returns Phase objects compatible with existing pipeline\n\n3. `run_mitigation_trial(model, tokenizer, problem, target_s, delta, phase_method) → Dict`\n   - Runs one problem under one condition\n   - phase_method ∈ {\"oracle\", \"heuristic\"}\n   - Returns per-phase II values and weighted aggregate\n\n**Reuse from existing pipeline**:\n- `load_model_and_tokenizer()` — unchanged\n- `stratified_sample_math500()` — take first 30\n- `InteractionIndexCalculator` — core II computation, unchanged\n- `segment_by_quantile()` + `to_phase_annotation()` — oracle phase labels\n- `_select_heads_to_mask()`, `_select_kv_to_evict()` — mask generation at arbitrary sparsity\n- `compute_conditioned_perplexities()` — per-phase PPL computation\n\n**New logic** (~150 lines):\n- Phase-aware sparsity allocation function\n- Heuristic phase assignment function\n- Main loop: for each problem × condition, generate phase-specific masks and compute II\n- Output JSON with per-problem, per-condition II values\n\n### 5.2 Data Flow\n\n```\nInput:\n  - Existing bf16 generation traces (if saved) OR re-generate (adds ~30min)\n  - Existing bf16 profiling results for uniform baseline comparison\n\nPipeline (per problem):\n  1. Generate trace → segment phases (oracle) / assign phases (heuristic)\n  2. For each phase:\n     a. Compute phase-specific sparsity level (compute-matched)\n     b. Generate importance masks at phase-specific level\n     c. Forward pass with phase masks, extract phase-token PPL\n     d. Compute phase II\n  3. Compute weighted aggregate II\n  4. Compare against uniform baseline II\n\nOutput:\n  - mitigation_pilot_results.json:\n    {config, per_problem: [{problem_id, uniform_ii, oracle_ii, heuristic_ii, recovery_pct, ...}]}\n  - Summary statistics + gate evaluation\n```\n\n### 5.3 Compatibility Notes\n\n- **Generation reuse**: If bf16 generation traces are saved in existing profiling output (check `profiling_results.json` for per-problem token sequences), reuse them. Otherwise re-generate — adds ~30min but ensures exact matching.\n- **Mask generation**: `_select_heads_to_mask` and `_select_kv_to_evict` accept arbitrary sparsity levels. No changes needed.\n- **Phase segmentation**: `segment_by_quantile` works on the sparsity trace from generation. Oracle uses this. Heuristic bypasses it.\n\n---\n\n## 6. GPU & Time Budget\n\n| Component | GPU Time | Notes |\n|-----------|----------|-------|\n| Model loading | 2 min | DeepSeek 7B bf16, single GPU |\n| Generation (if needed) | 30 min | 30 problems × ~60s each |\n| Oracle condition | 30 min | 30 problems × 2 sparsity × 3 phases × 3 forward passes |\n| Heuristic condition | 30 min | Same as oracle |\n| Analysis + bootstrap | 5 min | B=10000 on CPU |\n| **Total** | **~1.5h** (with re-generation) or **~1h** (with trace reuse) |\n\n**GPU**: 1× RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB. Can run on any of GPU 0-3 after c2_qw_n500 frees up GPU 2 or 3.\n\n**Timeline dependency**: plan_009 (c2_n500_deepseek, GPU 0+1) and plan_010 (c2_qw_n500, GPU 2+3) are currently running (~22h each). Once any GPU frees up, this pilot can start immediately. Expected availability: ~05-15 12:00 CST (based on c2_qw_n500 progress at ~8/500 as of 05-14 22:00).\n\n**Hard deadline**: Results needed by 05-22 for paper integration. With 1.5h runtime, scheduling is not a bottleneck — GPU availability is the sole constraint.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Expected Outcomes & Contingencies\n\n### Scenario A: Oracle recovery > 30% (PASS)\n\n- C5b enters paper as \"phase-aware allocation recovers X% of compound degradation (oracle upper bound)\"\n- If heuristic also recovers >10%, include both in paper as oracle/heuristic pair\n- One paragraph in §5 Results + one figure (bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic II)\n\n### Scenario B: Oracle recovery 10-30% (MARGINAL)\n\n- C5b enters Discussion (§6) as \"phase-aware allocation shows promising but modest recovery\"\n- Emphasize that even partial recovery validates the practical relevance of the phase-modulated severity finding\n- Frame full optimization as future work\n\n### Scenario C: Oracle recovery < 10% (FAIL)\n\n- C5b = future work, one sentence in Discussion\n- Possible explanations: (1) compound interaction at phase boundaries blurs the effect; (2) head pruning mask is global (can't be phase-specific); (3) KV eviction phase-awareness alone is insufficient\n- Core claims C1-C6 unaffected — finding stands without mitigation\n\n### Risk: Generation trace mismatch\n\nIf bf16 traces are not saved and re-generation produces different phase segmentations, uniform baseline II values will differ from existing n=50 data. Mitigation: always compare within the same run's generation (uniform vs oracle/heuristic computed from the same trace), never cross-run.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Output Artifacts\n\n| Artifact | Path | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| Results JSON | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_results.json` | Per-problem raw data |\n| Summary | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_summary.md` | Gate evaluation + key numbers |\n| Figure | `paper_figures/fig_mitigation_pilot.pdf` | Bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Handoff Checklist for Coding Worker\n\n- [ ] Check if bf16 profiling output contains saved generation traces (token IDs + attention sparsity per step)\n- [ ] Create `pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py` with the three functions specified in §5.1\n- [ ] Reuse `stratified_sample_math500(seed=42)[:30]` for consistent problem selection\n- [ ] Use δ=0.10 for phase-aware allocation, with s_trans capped at 0.50\n- [ ] Compute-match constraint must hold exactly per-problem (not averaged)\n- [ ] Output format: JSON with per-problem breakdown + aggregate summary\n- [ ] Register artifact after completion\n- [ ] Run on single GPU, ~1.5h total wall time\n"

Implementation Plan\n\n### 5.1 Code Changes\n\nCreate `run_mitigation_pilot.py` extending the existing profiling pipeline:\n\n```\npacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py\n```\n\n**Key functions to add**:\n\n1. `compute_phase_aware_sparsity(target_s, delta, phase_token_counts) → Dict[str, float]`\n   - Returns per-phase sparsity levels, compute-matched\n   - Caps s_trans at 0.50\n\n2. `assign_heuristic_phases(seq_len, prompt_len) → List[Phase]`\n   - Token position ratio → phase assignment\n   - Returns Phase objects compatible with existing pipeline\n\n3. `run_mitigation_trial(model, tokenizer, problem, target_s, delta, phase_method) → Dict`\n   - Runs one problem under one condition\n   - phase_method ∈ {\"oracle\", \"heuristic\"}\n   - Returns per-phase II values and weighted aggregate\n\n**Reuse from existing pipeline**:\n- `load_model_and_tokenizer()` — unchanged\n- `stratified_sample_math500()` — take first 30\n- `InteractionIndexCalculator` — core II computation, unchanged\n- `segment_by_quantile()` + `to_phase_annotation()` — oracle phase labels\n- `_select_heads_to_mask()`, `_select_kv_to_evict()` — mask generation at arbitrary sparsity\n- `compute_conditioned_perplexities()` — per-phase PPL computation\n\n**New logic** (~150 lines):\n- Phase-aware sparsity allocation function\n- Heuristic phase assignment function\n- Main loop: for each problem × condition, generate phase-specific masks and compute II\n- Output JSON with per-problem, per-condition II values\n\n### 5.2 Data Flow\n\n```\nInput:\n  - Existing bf16 generation traces (if saved) OR re-generate (adds ~30min)\n  - Existing bf16 profiling results for uniform baseline comparison\n\nPipeline (per problem):\n  1. Generate trace → segment phases (oracle) / assign phases (heuristic)\n  2. For each phase:\n     a. Compute phase-specific sparsity level (compute-matched)\n     b. Generate importance masks at phase-specific level\n     c. Forward pass with phase masks, extract phase-token PPL\n     d. Compute phase II\n  3. Compute weighted aggregate II\n  4. Compare against uniform baseline II\n\nOutput:\n  - mitigation_pilot_results.json:\n    {config, per_problem: [{problem_id, uniform_ii, oracle_ii, heuristic_ii, recovery_pct, ...}]}\n  - Summary statistics + gate evaluation\n```\n\n### 5.3 Compatibility Notes\n\n- **Generation reuse**: If bf16 generation traces are saved in existing profiling output (check `profiling_results.json` for per-problem token sequences), reuse them. Otherwise re-generate — adds ~30min but ensures exact matching.\n- **Mask generation**: `_select_heads_to_mask` and `_select_kv_to_evict` accept arbitrary sparsity levels. No changes needed.\n- **Phase segmentation**: `segment_by_quantile` works on the sparsity trace from generation. Oracle uses this. Heuristic bypasses it.\n\n---\n\n## 6. GPU & Time Budget\n\n| Component | GPU Time | Notes |\n|-----------|----------|-------|\n| Model loading | 2 min | DeepSeek 7B bf16, single GPU |\n| Generation (if needed) | 30 min | 30 problems × ~60s each |\n| Oracle condition | 30 min | 30 problems × 2 sparsity × 3 phases × 3 forward passes |\n| Heuristic condition | 30 min | Same as oracle |\n| Analysis + bootstrap | 5 min | B=10000 on CPU |\n| **Total** | **~1.5h** (with re-generation) or **~1h** (with trace reuse) |\n\n**GPU**: 1× RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB. Can run on any of GPU 0-3 after c2_qw_n500 frees up GPU 2 or 3.\n\n**Timeline dependency**: plan_009 (c2_n500_deepseek, GPU 0+1) and plan_010 (c2_qw_n500, GPU 2+3) are currently running (~22h each). Once any GPU frees up, this pilot can start immediately. Expected availability: ~05-15 12:00 CST (based on c2_qw_n500 progress at ~8/500 as of 05-14 22:00).\n\n**Hard deadline**: Results needed by 05-22 for paper integration. With 1.5h runtime, scheduling is not a bottleneck — GPU availability is the sole constraint.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Expected Outcomes & Contingencies\n\n### Scenario A: Oracle recovery > 30% (PASS)\n\n- C5b enters paper as \"phase-aware allocation recovers X% of compound degradation (oracle upper bound)\"\n- If heuristic also recovers >10%, include both in paper as oracle/heuristic pair\n- One paragraph in §5 Results + one figure (bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic II)\n\n### Scenario B: Oracle recovery 10-30% (MARGINAL)\n\n- C5b enters Discussion (§6) as \"phase-aware allocation shows promising but modest recovery\"\n- Emphasize that even partial recovery validates the practical relevance of the phase-modulated severity finding\n- Frame full optimization as future work\n\n### Scenario C: Oracle recovery < 10% (FAIL)\n\n- C5b = future work, one sentence in Discussion\n- Possible explanations: (1) compound interaction at phase boundaries blurs the effect; (2) head pruning mask is global (can't be phase-specific); (3) KV eviction phase-awareness alone is insufficient\n- Core claims C1-C6 unaffected — finding stands without mitigation\n\n### Risk: Generation trace mismatch\n\nIf bf16 traces are not saved and re-generation produces different phase segmentations, uniform baseline II values will differ from existing n=50 data. Mitigation: always compare within the same run's generation (uniform vs oracle/heuristic computed from the same trace), never cross-run.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Output Artifacts\n\n| Artifact | Path | Description |\n|----------|------|-------------|\n| Results JSON | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_results.json` | Per-problem raw data |\n| Summary | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_summary.md` | Gate evaluation + key numbers |\n| Figure | `paper_figures/fig_mitigation_pilot.pdf` | Bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Handoff Checklist for Coding Worker\n\n- [ ] Check if bf16 profiling output contains saved generation traces (token IDs + attention sparsity per step)\n- [ ] Create `pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py` with the three functions specified in §5.1\n- [ ] Reuse `stratified_sample_math500(seed=42)[:30]` for consistent problem selection\n- [ ] Use δ=0.10 for phase-aware allocation, with s_trans capped at 0.50\n- [ ] Compute-match constraint must hold exactly per-problem (not averaged)\n- [ ] Output format: JSON with per-problem breakdown + aggregate summary\n- [ ] Register artifact after completion\n- [ ] Run on single GPU, ~1.5h total wall time\n",

  }

方案已写入 `artifacts/c5b_pilot_design.md` 并注册 (`art_20260514_4d09f3`)。

核心设计要点：

**条件设计**：
- **Uniform baseline** — 复用现有 bf16 importance 数据，零 GPU 成本
- **Oracle** — 用真实 quantile phase labels 分配 sparsity：execution 相降 δ=0.10，transition 相加，总量 compute-matched
- **Heuristic** — token position ratio (pos/seq_len 三等分) 作为最简 phase proxy，零成本下界

**规模**：n=30 problems × {s025, s030} × 3 conditions，仅 DeepSeek（信号最强），单 GPU ~1.5h

**评估**：per-problem weighted II 的 recovery %，paired Wilcoxon + bootstrap CI (B=10000)。Gate: oracle recovery >30% 任一 sparsity level。

**实现**：新建 `run_mitigation_pilot.py`，复用现有 `InteractionIndexCalculator` 和 phase segmentation，新增 ~150 行（phase-aware allocation + heuristic assignment + main loop）。

**时间线**：等 GPU 2/3 空闲（c2_qw_n500 完成后，预计 05-15 中午），1.5h 跑完，远在 05-22 deadline 之前。
